## Consequential corrections

- Scan 023, tetrahedron drawing: Four vertices and six edges agree. The second tracing preserves the incomplete upper arc as two separated parts, the partial retracing of the central edge, the three small triangular edge marks, and the two wings of the broad external arrow. Retain beta/question-mark doubt at the left; no certain letter is inferred from the drawing. (medium).
- Scan 023, small mark left of tetrahedron: \unsure{\beta|?} (low).
- Scan 023, first canceled count: \deleted{\deleted{6}\inserted{4}\text{ hexagones}} (high).
- Scan 023, isolated group: \unsure{G|A|a}_{\unsure{4}} (low).
- Scan 023, canceled group symbol: \deleted{\unsure{\widetilde{\mathfrak S}_3}} (low).
- Scan 023, separator between group lines: \mu_6\times(\mathbb Z/2\mathbb Z)^2\\\mu_4\times(\mathbb Z/2\mathbb Z)^2 (high).
- Scan 023, triangular lattice: The nine horizontal levels and eight triangular bands agree. The independent tracing more closely follows the unequal vertices, explicit gaps and individual hatching. It also follows the descending leftward end of the right bold oblique and the curved end strokes, whereas the first version regularized several ends into generic arrows. Adopt the literal paths and omit the overlapping formula fragments, which are already transcribed separately. (medium).
- Scan 023, pencil polyhedron: The two versions preserve the same principal polyhedral outline. The crop additionally shows disconnected short internal strokes, partially retraced edges, and V-like endpoint strokes at the central and bottom vertices. The second paths reproduce those details without assigning a new direction to the V-like marks. The partial heading belongs to the preceding text and is not repeated. (medium).
- Scan 023, small parallelogram: All four sides are heavily retraced, the top has a narrow doubled trace, and a separate thin stroke lies outside the right edge. The lower-left dark patch is larger than the lower-right corner patch. Adopt these visible weights and partial retracings; do not add a vertex label. (medium).
- Scan 023, three-sided open drawing: The top and right strokes stop short of each other. The left shaft ends in a downward hook/head, with a second disconnected angled stroke at its lower right; the upper-left also has a detached angled mark. The second literal paths preserve these gaps and paired marks better than the first generic arrow and short tick. (medium).
- Scan 023, square lattice: Five long verticals, six uneven horizontal levels, and the diagonals are visible. The independent paths retain the open top and bottom sections, small left-of-center arrow-like strokes, separate top/right corner marks, and low right vertical extension. These replace the overly regular generic arrows in the first version. No algebraic labels or unstated orientation are supplied. (medium).
- Scan 023, lower-left rotation parallelogram: The outline has a gap below the upper-right dark vertex, an open lower-right ring with a short outward stroke, and a curved rotation mark with visible interruptions before its detached upper head. Adopt these gaps and the literal head instead of the continuous first arrow. (medium).
- Scan 023, square count correction: \unsure{\deleted{2}\inserted{4}|\deleted{4}\inserted{2}}\text{ carrés}\\8\text{ arêtes}\\4\text{ sommets} (medium).
- Scan 023, lower-left object in framed diagram: \unsure{E_Q|\overline E_Q} (medium).
- Scan 023, lower-right object in framed diagram: \unsure{E_H|\overline E_H} (medium).
- Scan 023, upper curved arrows of framed diagram: \arrow[r,bend left]\arrow[l,bend left] (high).
- Scan 023, vertical connections in framed diagram: The two upper curved arrows and lower rightward arrow agree. Each vertical connection visibly contains two parallel dark stems and both an upper and lower head; preserve both stems rather than merging them into one shaft. Their semantic status as separate maps versus graphic retracing is not asserted. The upper-left E is heavily retraced, insufficient to establish a distinct double-struck alphabet. Each lower E has only three horizontals in total, so its detached top remains ambiguous between the letter top and an overbar. Keep those doubts and both epsilon readings. (medium).
- Scan 024, Drawing sheet as a whole: The drawing sheet is reproduced in TikZ, with detailed corrections listed separately. (high).

Keep those doubts and both epsilon readings. crops/second/148_scan023/B023-20.jxl (1200, 1942, 1540, 2200) | medium |

The current index is 4-shaped and heavily retraced; its prior state remains unknown. crops/second/148_scan023/B023-08.jxl (235, 980, 342, 1083) | low |

### Still unresolved

**M0246** — Reading: `\unsure{\circlearrowleft^{3}|\mathfrak S_3}`. The sign has an outer curved loop, inner curl and a small raised 3-like mark. The loop does not securely establish an arrowhead or the alternative script letter. Alternatives: \mathfrak S_3. Confidence: low.

**M0247** — Reading: `\unsure{\circlearrowleft|G}`. The sign has a rounded enclosing stroke and a short inward stroke; the latter does not securely distinguish the proposed G and circular-arrow readings. Alternatives: G. Confidence: low.

**M0248** — Reading: `\unsure{b}`. There is a dark upright/looped mark beside the left tetrahedral vertex, but the filled vertex and overlapping edge obscure the bowl needed to assert b. Confidence: low.

**M0249** — Reading: `\unsure{c}`. The short curved label is merged with dark adjoining vertex strokes; its opening cannot be isolated securely. Confidence: low.

**M0252** — Reading: `\unsure{0|\odot}`. The oval contains a dark central mark and short upright stroke; both a numerical label with overlap and a circled point remain possible. Alternatives: \odot. Confidence: low.

**M0256** — Reading: `\unsure{avec}`. The initial oval and following low joined strokes are partly crossed by the diagram. The final sequence is compatible with vec but not clear enough to remove doubt. Confidence: low.

**M0262** — Reading: `\unsure{17|14}`. The second digit combines a long descending 7-like stroke with an angular 4-like contour. The order in which they were drawn is not secure. Alternatives: 14. Confidence: low.

**M0263** — Reading: `\unsure{cartes|points|ponts}`. The descender-like first stroke, rounded next stroke, central tall stroke and low ending do not fully distinguish the alternatives. Alternatives: points|ponts. Confidence: low.

**M0264** — Reading: `\unsure{on|ou|or}`. The short word is present, with an initial loop and joined ending; the ending can be read as n, u or r. Alternatives: ou|or. Confidence: low.

**U24-QUATRE** — Reading: `\deleted{\unsure{quatre}}`. Heavy cancellation and inserted trois obscure part of the word. Confidence: low.

**U24-F** — Reading: `\deleted{\unsure{F}}`. Cancelled strokes do not securely establish F or an alternative letter. Confidence: low.

**U24-TYPE-PLACEMENT** — Reading: `\inserted{de type \((0,4)\)}`. Phrase is clear, but exact point of attachment in the linear text is not explicit. Confidence: medium.

### Still unresolved

**Prefix before revêtements** — Reading: `\unsure{\(\deleted{\mathbf Z}n-\)|\(\deleted{\mathbf Z/n}\)}`. The overtraced Z-like figure, possible slash and cancellation extent cannot be separated securely. Alternatives: \(\deleted{\mathbf Z}n-\), \(\deleted{\mathbf Z/n}\). Confidence: low.

**Deletion status of revêtements** — Reading: `\unsure{revêtements|\deleted{revêtements}}`. The joined baseline and heavy final strokes can be retracing or cancellation; no distinct full-word strike is separable. Alternatives: revêtements, \deleted{revêtements}. Confidence: low.

**Word after ramifiés** — Reading: `\unsure{seulement}`. The medial letters are highly compressed and partly intersected by the large loop; every letter is not independently legible. Alternatives: seulement. Confidence: medium.

**First word of the struck phrase** — Reading: `\deleted{\unsure{variétés} algébriques d}`. The horizontal cancellation hides the low letter bodies in variétés; the word cannot be released from doubt letter by letter. Alternatives: variétés. Confidence: low.

### Still unresolved

**Initial numeral of separate left count** — Reading: `2`. The numeral is overtraced and includes a lower loop and extra angled stroke. A prior numeral cannot be separated securely; no definite deleted digit has been invented. Alternatives: . Confidence: low.

**Beginning of lower struck line** — Reading: `\unsure{1\times4|\text{dont }4|4\times4}`. The cancellation crosses the lower strokes, so the upright and crossed angled form cannot be resolved into a certain word or product. Alternatives: \text{dont }4, 4\times4. Confidence: low.

**Faint underlying drawings** — Reading: `Pale contours retained in TikZ`. Coherent ovals, polygonal edges and vertex loops are visible, but their faintness limits the precision of individual contour junctions. Alternatives: . Confidence: medium.

### Still unresolved

**I6 three group symbols** — Reading: `\unsure{\mathfrak S|\mathfrak G}_J,\ \unsure{\mathfrak S|\mathfrak G}_3,\ \unsure{\mathfrak S|\mathfrak G}_3`. All three have a wavy upper stroke and broad lower loop. Cross-comparison with scan033 programme1 confirms the decorated alphabet, distinct from the adjacent plain G; S versus fraktur G remains unresolved. Alternatives: \mathfrak S, \mathfrak G. Confidence: low.

**upper compatibility symbol** — Reading: `\unsure{\vec Q|\vec G}`. The lower loop and right continuation do not securely separate Q from G. Alternatives: \vec Q, \vec G. Confidence: low.

**lower compatibility symbol** — Reading: `\unsure{\vec Q|\vec G}`. The retraced loop remains open to Q or G. Alternatives: \vec Q, \vec G. Confidence: low.

**fBs accent** — Reading: `\unsure{\mathrm{Bit\acute et}|\mathrm{Bitet}}`. The short upper stroke is faint in this occurrence; the lower key accent was adjudicated separately. Alternatives: Bitét, Bitet. Confidence: low.

**II2 struck word** — Reading: `\deleted{\text{\unsure{repères}}}`. The separate lower word is struck; not every letter can be distinguished from its crossing strokes. Alternatives: repères, unread canceled word. Confidence: low.

**II2prime struck word** — Reading: `\deleted{\text{\unsure{repères}}}`. The entire word after 4 sommets is crossed; its internal letters remain partly obscured. Alternatives: repères, unread canceled word. Confidence: low.

**II1 canceled group material** — Reading: `\deleted{\unsure{1}}`. The overwritten dark group entry cannot be read letter by letter. Alternatives: 1, unread canceled symbol. Confidence: low.

**II1 figure P labels** — Reading: `\unsure{P},\ \unsure{\bar P}`. The live P-like labels are heavily retraced; separate earlier letter forms cannot be securely recovered. Alternatives: P and bar P with unread overwritten strokes. Confidence: low.

**tau pi omega faint label** — Reading: `\unsure{\tau_{\pi,\omega}}`. The upper pencil label is faint and partly overwritten by the main annotation. Alternatives: \tau_{\pi,\omega}, unread tau subscript. Confidence: low.

**III2prime repère formula** — Reading: `\unsure{\mathfrak X|\mathbb J}\unsure{(}I)`. The X-like letter and a possible left parenthesis cannot be separated confidently; one closing parenthesis is visible. Alternatives: \mathfrak X(I), \mathfrak X I), \mathbb J(I). Confidence: low.

**III2prime group blob** — Reading: `\unsure{\deleted{*}}`. The dark material after the quotient does not establish its original glyph or conclusively distinguish cancellation from retracing. Alternatives: canceled star-like mark, unread live or canceled strokes. Confidence: low.

**III2prime first figure overwritten labels** — Reading: `\unsure{\alpha'},\ \unsure{\beta},\ \unsure{\beta'}`. The top rear and lower two labels contain distinct overlapping dark strokes; the prior forms are not secure. Alternatives: alpha-prime, beta, beta-prime with unread underforms. Confidence: low.

**level0 middle index** — Reading: `\mathrm{III}_{\unsure{4|1}}`. On the reopened existing crop the small index has an angled head, cross-stroke and descending stem resembling the 4 in the same table. The repeated ink does not wholly exclude 1; 4 is retained first and no prime is added. Alternatives: 4, 1. Confidence: low.

**punctuation after Aut+** — Reading: `\unsure{;|,}`. A dot and a long curved descending stroke do not securely distinguish semicolon from comma. Alternatives: ;, ,. Confidence: low.

**parameter NB overwritten relation** — Reading: `\unsure{\simeq}`. The visible relation is tilde over a horizontal stroke, but separately crossed underlying strokes are unreadable. Alternatives: simeq with unread prior relation. Confidence: low.

| Total formula, current multiplier | \unsure{11} | 11 | 11 | Two current long uprights, each with a high angled approach, are visible in the overwritten multiplier. | Total formula, underlying multiplier |  |  | \deleted{\unsure{12}}\inserted{11} | The current second upright overlays an older curved head and broad lower stroke that admit 2. | Programme 1, adjective after syst. | fondamental | fondamentales | fondamental | The word ends in one tall looped l and its short exit. | Programme 1, entre before 14 | \deleted{entre} | \uncertain{entre} | \deleted{entre} | A heavy horizontal strike crosses the entire short word before 14. Its cancellation is visible independently of the later cancelled phrase. crops/148_scan033_programme1.jxl (771, 242, 1621, 536) | high |

### Still unresolved

**1020 group, former subtotal** — Reading: `\deleted{\unsure{900}}`. Diagonal cancellation covers parts of the old digits; the 9-like loop and following round forms are not all plain. Alternatives: 900, other cancelled numeral. Confidence: low.

**1020 group, overwritten third label** — Reading: `\unsure{\tau'_r|\tau_r^1}`. The superposed upper-right strokes prevent a secure prime/exponent decision. Alternatives: \tau'_r, \tau_r^1. Confidence: low.

**1020 group, cancelled fifth-row equality** — Reading: `\deleted{\unsure{2\times60=2\times60}}`. Heavy blackening obscures the older factors below the clear replacement. The inherited reading remains doubtful. Alternatives: 2\times60=2\times60, another cancelled equality. Confidence: low.

**Total, cancelled former value** — Reading: `\deleted{\unsure{1580|1500}}`. Surviving counters favor 8 in the third position, but not every older digit is clear under the strike. Alternatives: 1580, 1500. Confidence: medium.

**Total, former multiplier** — Reading: `\deleted{\unsure{12}}`. Older curved head and base overlap the current two uprights; 12 is plausible but not securely separated. Alternatives: 12, another overwritten two-digit value. Confidence: low.

**Programme 1, article before syst.** — Reading: `\unsure{les|un}`. The tall initial favors l, but the compressed remainder does not completely exclude C’s un. Alternatives: les, un. Confidence: medium.

**Programme 1, cancelled word below orbites** — Reading: `\deleted{\unsure{paquets} de}`. Two descenders support p/q, but the intervening letters are crossed. The final de is separately visible. Alternatives: paquets de, another cancelled word followed by de. Confidence: low.

**Programme 1, acting group** — Reading: `\unsure{\mathfrak S_K|\mathfrak G_K}`. The large curved glyph permits S and G; no alphabetic decision is made. Alternatives: \mathfrak S_K, \mathfrak G_K. Confidence: medium.

**Programme 1, word before 542** — Reading: `\unsure{aux|avec}`. The short run favors aux, but its joins do not rule out avec. Alternatives: aux, avec. Confidence: medium.

**Cancelled start between items 1 and 2** — Reading: `\deleted{2) \unsure{Réduire}}`. The initial capital and some tall strokes survive, but the crossing stroke hides the remainder; C’s proposed word is retained as a doubt. Alternatives: Réduire, another cancelled word beginning R. Confidence: low.

**Cancelled paragraph, initial short fragment** — Reading: `\unsure{qui} de type`. A q-like descender and a dot survive. The word before de type remains crossed and uncertain. Alternatives: qui, another short word. Confidence: low.

**Cancelled paragraph, verb after second qui** — Reading: `\unsure{reviennent|revient}`. Several low humps favor the plural, but their tops lie under the cancellation. Alternatives: reviennent, revient. Confidence: medium.

**Cancelled paragraph, word after twist** — Reading: `\unsure{relationnel|relativement}`. The low middle arches favor relationnel; the ending is not plain enough to remove relativement. Alternatives: relationnel, relativement. Confidence: medium.

**Cancelled paragraph, beginning of last line** — Reading: `\unsure{un Autom.}`. The lower frame stroke passes through this word group, hiding parts of the abbreviated ending. Alternatives: un Autom., another cancelled abbreviation. Confidence: low.

**Programme 3, cancelled adjective** — Reading: `\deleted{\unsure{gauches}}`. Initial descending loop and a later tall stroke are compatible with gauches, but the horizontal strike obscures most small letters. Alternatives: gauches, another cancelled adjective. Confidence: low.

### Still unresolved

**title, cancelled courbe** — Reading: `\deleted{\unsure{courbe}}`. The strike obscures the middle letters; beginning c, ascender b, and final e support but do not fully certify courbe. Alternatives: courbe. Confidence: low.

**title, characteristic phrase** — Reading: `\unsure{de car. \(0\)}`. The opening de is overtraced; the more legible remainder does not certify the whole phrase. Alternatives: de car. \(0\). Confidence: low.

**cancelled order-5 group, initial stroke** — Reading: `\deleted{\(\unsure{\nu}\,g\) d’ordre \(5\)}`. An extra nu-like stroke precedes g under the cancellation; its identity is not secure. Alternatives: \nu, unidentified stroke. Confidence: medium.

**cancelled il est** — Reading: `\deleted{\unsure{il est}}`. The cancellation runs through the middle and terminal letters. The original doubt remains. Alternatives: il est. Confidence: low.

**case 2, cancelled initial letter** — Reading: `\deleted{\(\unsure{K|\Sigma}=\operatorname{card}I^g=1\)}`. The crossed and entangled initial cannot securely be separated into both K arms; a Sigma-like outline remains possible. No separate superscript g is visible before the equals sign. Alternatives: K, \Sigma. Confidence: medium.

**case 2, pole symbol** — Reading: `\(\unsure{\infty}\)`. Compact intersecting loops are overtraced, especially at the left. Alternatives: \infty. Confidence: medium.

**case 3, cancelled initial symbol** — Reading: `\deleted{\(\unsure{\Sigma|K}\)}`. A Sigma-like angular outline survives, but the crossed strokes prevent an unqualified identification. Alternatives: \Sigma, K. Confidence: medium.

**association, cancelled symbol after Sigma_J** — Reading: `\(\Sigma_J\deleted{\unsure{\Sigma}}\)`. Several crossing loops obscure the small angular symbol after the live Sigma_J. Alternatives: \Sigma. Confidence: low.

**association, inclusion formula exponent** — Reading: `\(P_J\subset\Sigma_J^{\unsure{g}}\)`. The small loop and descending stroke on the right are not securely separable into exponent g and subscript J. Alternatives: \Sigma_J^g, \Sigma_J. Confidence: low.

**ending, field letter** — Reading: `\(\unsure{k|K}=\mathbb C\)`. An overtraced stem with joined right branches does not securely distinguish case. Alternatives: k, K. Confidence: medium.

**third margin, initial word** — Reading: `\unsure{Partition}`. The initial is p/P-like but the linked middle strokes do not certify the proposed word. Alternatives: Partition. Confidence: low.

**third margin, word before ou symétries** — Reading: `\unsure{cardinaux|valeurs}`. The raised mark, looped ascender, and low ending do not securely distinguish the two witness candidates. Alternatives: cardinaux, valeurs. Confidence: low.

### Still unresolved

**a: cancelled opening fragment** — Reading: `\deleted{\unsure{Le cas|un cas}}`. The long strike obscures some short joining strokes. The tall initial favours Le, but not every struck letter is independently clear. Alternatives: Le cas, un cas. Confidence: medium.

**left oblique margin: cancelled initial word** — Reading: `\deleted{\unsure{pour}}`. The descending first stroke and remaining low run support pour, but the strike hides the internal joins. Alternatives: pour. Confidence: medium.

**c: tiny cancelled fragment after i.e.** — Reading: `\deleted{\unsure{?}}`. The dense retracing does not reveal the former character. Alternatives: ?, unidentified cancelled character. Confidence: low.

**c: first word of insertion** — Reading: `\unsure{Soit}`. The initial and interior letters have been heavily retraced; Soit remains the readable proposal rather than a secure resolution. Alternatives: Soit. Confidence: medium.

**c: middle of first cancelled line** — Reading: `\deleted{\unsure{comme repère}}`. The rising strike runs through the letter bodies. The ascenders and descenders admit the phrase but do not expose every letter. Alternatives: comme repère. Confidence: medium.

**c: inserted word over muni d’** — Reading: `\unsure{admettant}`. The raised word is cramped against the cancelled line and its internal joins remain uncertain. Alternatives: admettant. Confidence: medium.

**c: cancelled sign before involutif** — Reading: `\deleted{\(\unsure{\sigma}\)}`. A dense crossed loop remains; the original sigma cannot be isolated. Alternatives: \sigma, unidentified cancelled sign. Confidence: low.

**lower chart: live realizations label** — Reading: `\unsure{modulaires|spéciales}`. The low initial humps favour modulaires, but the rest overlaps cancelled writing. Alternatives: modulaires, spéciales. Confidence: medium.

**lower chart: cancelled word under realizations** — Reading: `\deleted{\unsure{polyédriques}}`. Heavy crossing strokes obscure the interior letters and the exact overlap with the upper label. Alternatives: polyédriques. Confidence: low.

**lower chart: mark following first 60** — Reading: `\unsure{(|c}`. A small isolated right-opening curve has no visible closing mate; whether it is punctuation or a letter is unsettled. Alternatives: (, c. Confidence: low.

**lower chart: cancelled caption** — Reading: `\deleted{\unsure{tétraèdre-pyr|tétraèdre-pyramide}}`. The right end is a short crossed run after a p descender; no full ending can be securely separated. Alternatives: tétraèdre-pyr, tétraèdre-pyramide, tétraédrales followed by unread text. Confidence: low.

**lower chart: first word of lower parenthesis** — Reading: `\unsure{tétraèdre|pentagonales}`. Retouching masks several joins. The taller stems favour tétraèdre, while the competing H reading is retained for blind review. Alternatives: tétraèdre, pentagonales. Confidence: medium.

**lower chart: isolated cancelled words at left** — Reading: `\deleted{\unsure{les types}}`. The cancellation is too dense for a complete letter-by-letter recovery. Alternatives: les types. Confidence: low.

**NB: earlier state under the last factor 2** — Reading: `2`. The present 2 is clear and heavily retraced. An earlier numeral cannot be separated from the thickened strokes; no former digit is supplied. Alternatives: retraced 2, 2 over an unidentified earlier digit. Confidence: medium.

**bottom sketch: central glyph** — Reading: `\unsure{\sigma}`. The open rounded form with an inward upper-right hook does not securely identify its alphabet or status. Alternatives: \sigma, 0, 3, curved orientation mark. Confidence: low.

### Still unresolved

**first display, cancelled fraction** — Reading: `\deleted{\unsure{\frac{\xi}{\eta}}}`. Heavy crossing obscures parts of both stacked signs. Alternatives: \frac{\xi}{\eta}. Confidence: low.

**word after iso.** — Reading: `\uline{\unsure{un|aux}}`. Low beginning and linked terminal strokes do not certify a complete word; the un-like form may be unfinished. The visible line is below the letters; a cancellation cannot be securely asserted. Alternatives: un, aux. Confidence: low.

**definition annotation** — Reading: `\overset{\unsure{\mathrm{df}}}{=}`. The annotation is above the equals sign but is crossed by descending ink; both letters are not independently secure. Alternatives: \mathrm{df}. Confidence: medium.

**basis paragraph, cancelled de** — Reading: `\deleted{\unsure{de}}`. The strike obscures most of the low letters. Alternatives: de. Confidence: low.

**distinctness, raised cluster after mu** — Reading: `\unsure{\mu^2|\mu}\ne\pm2`. The raised black cluster may be a 2 or a cancelled exponent. Mathematical equivalence is not used to force its state. Alternatives: \mu^2, \mu, \mu^{\deleted{2}}. Confidence: low.

**cancelled Les** — Reading: `\deleted{\unsure{Les}}`. The L is visible but e/s are entangled with the strike. Alternatives: Les. Confidence: low.

**first radical, closing parenthesis** — Reading: `\sqrt{\tfrac12\left(\mu\pm\sqrt{\mu^2-4}\right.}`. The formula touches the lower paper edge; no closing parenthesis is readable in the surviving area. It is not supplied. Alternatives: . Confidence: medium.

### Still unresolved

**title, sign after holomorphe** — Reading: `\unsure{à|:}`. An upper angled mark and lower heavy loop/dot do not securely distinguish an accented a from a colon. Alternatives: à, :. Confidence: medium.

**sum membership target** — Reading: `\unsure{t_2|T_2}`. A strong crossbar excludes an ordinary L, but the protruding stem and heavy foot leave lowercase t versus capital T uncertain. Alternatives: t_2, T_2. Confidence: medium.

**live condition, sign in first parentheses** — Reading: `(\xi\unsure{\wedge|+}\eta)^2-4\xi\wedge\eta\ne0`. The small middle sign is peaked, unlike the clear plus in the preceding sum, but its reduced shape could include a crossing. The later product wedge is clear and is retained. Alternatives: \wedge, +. Confidence: medium.

**short cancellation after discriminant** — Reading: `\deleted{\unsure{un}}`. The strike obscures most of the low letters; un remains only a tentative recovery. Alternatives: un. Confidence: low.

**cancelled expansion after Notons** — Reading: `\deleted{$\unsure{\xi^2+\eta^2-2\xi\wedge\eta\ne}$}`. The raised shape after the first xi and several crossings are not independently plain. The eta-square, expanded layout and peaked wedge between the last factors support the retained tentative expression. Alternatives: \xi^2+\eta^2-2\xi\wedge\eta\ne. Confidence: low.

**abbreviated element** — Reading: `\unsure{élt}`. The word is visibly abbreviated, but the initial accent/letter is not secure enough to remove the existing doubt. Alternatives: élt, elt. Confidence: medium.

**final cancelled square** — Reading: `\deleted{$\unsure{(\xi+\eta)^2}$}`. The outer parentheses and raised 2 survive; the central xi-plus-eta strokes are partly hidden by the heavy cancellation. The formula gains doubt without a certainty-only changes row. Alternatives: (\xi+\eta)^2. Confidence: medium.

**middle graph compound ending** — Reading: `\unsure{bipondérés|bipondéré}`. A small terminal curve favors plural s but is compressed. Alternatives: bipondérés, bipondéré. Confidence: medium.

**middle graph canceled word** — Reading: `\deleted{\uuline{\unsure{marqués|marqué}}}`. The deletion crosses the final letters, while two underline strokes survive. Alternatives: marqués, marqué. Confidence: medium.

**relative-twist canceled word** — Reading: `\deleted{\unsure{point|foncteur}}`. The p-like descender and short body favor point, but the deletion prevents every letter being read. Alternatives: point, foncteur. Confidence: low.

**last paragraph insertion** — Reading: `\inserted{\unsure{ou de recollement}}`. Ou de is clear; the ending after recol(l)-like strokes is compressed. Alternatives: ou de recollement, ou de followed by an unresolved word. Confidence: low.

**last word of sloping paragraph** — Reading: `\unsure{importante}`. The initial and medial strokes fit importante, but the ending is not fully secure. Alternatives: importante, unresolved ending of important-. Confidence: low.

### Still unresolved

**header initial** — Reading: `\unsure{les|Les}`. Bare tall initial stroke does not securely distinguish lower-case l from capital L. Alternatives: les, Les. Confidence: medium.

**header Tour(s)** — Reading: `\unsure{Tours|Tour}`. Heavy final zigzag favors s but may be a retouched r exit. Alternatives: Tours, Tour. Confidence: medium.

**struck header noun** — Reading: `\deleted{\unsure{systèmes|système} de groupoïdes}`. The terminal s-like stroke lies on the cancellation. Alternatives: systèmes, système. Confidence: low.

**interlinear short word** — Reading: `\unsure{réalis.}`. Tall l favors réalis.; the compressed first letters remain uncertain. Alternatives: réalis., ou autres. Confidence: medium.

**struck représentant** — Reading: `\deleted{\unsure{représentant}}`. The heavy strike and edge compression prevent certification of every letter. Alternatives: représentant. Confidence: low.

**struck capitals in prelude** — Reading: `\deleted{\unsure{\mathrm{RTD}}}`. Three capital-sized remnants are crossed by diagonal hatching; RTD remains tentative. Alternatives: \mathrm{RTD}. Confidence: low.

**struck word in top caption** — Reading: `\deleted{\unsure{géod.}}`. The first ascender and descending loop survive, but the middle letters are covered. Alternatives: géod.. Confidence: low.

**struck differentiable middle word** — Reading: `\deleted{pts \unsure{trous} pointés)}`. Two horizontal cancellation strokes obscure the middle word. Alternatives: trous. Confidence: low.

**lower differentiable cancellation** — Reading: `\deleted{(avec \unsure{peu de|presque} pts marqués)}`. Short p-e-u and d-e groups are favored, but the cancelling line covers the joins. Alternatives: peu de, presque. Confidence: medium.

**left note first abbreviation** — Reading: `\unsure{drs.|dirs.}`. The compressed middle may include an i; not expanded in the text. Alternatives: drs., dirs.. Confidence: medium.

**left note last abbreviation** — Reading: `\unsure{qconq.|qcq.}`. The number of rounded middle letters is uncertain. Alternatives: qconq., qcq.. Confidence: medium.

**analytic border initial glyph** — Reading: `\unsure{\deleted{g}|g}toriques`. A g-like form precedes toriques; the small upper cross may be cancellation or overtracing. Alternatives: \deleted{g}toriques, gtoriques. Confidence: low.

**analytic margin whole formula** — Reading: `\unsure{\mathbb C^\infty?}`. Doubled C is clear; raised looped and question-like marks are not securely identified. Infinity is a tentative witness reading, not a settled exponent. Alternatives: \mathbb C^\infty?, \mathbb C^{?}. Confidence: low.

**complex-algebraic propagation phrase** — Reading: `\unsure{de cercles|des cercles|du cercle}`. The tall near-final l favors cercle(s); short preposition and final s are compressed. Alternatives: de cercles, des cercles, du cercle. Confidence: medium.

**rational-algebraic first word** — Reading: `\unsure{propagation}`. Compressed letter joins favor propagation but cannot all be certified. Alternatives: propagation, pour opération, pour y obtenir. Confidence: medium.

**rational-algebraic second line** — Reading: `\unsure{descente|descendant}`. Initial d and final t-e favor descente, but the middle is compressed. Alternatives: descente, descendant. Confidence: medium.

**Gal initial state** — Reading: `\unsure{\mathrm{Gal}|\deleted{\mathrm G}\mathrm{al}}/\overline{\mathbb Q}`. G is heavily overtraced; al and the barred denominator Q are clear. Retouching versus cancellation of G is unresolved. Alternatives: \mathrm{Gal}/\overline{\mathbb Q}, \deleted{\mathrm G}\mathrm{al}/\overline{\mathbb Q}. Confidence: medium.

**combinatorial abbreviation** — Reading: `\unsure{quadr.|qcq.}`. The descending q is clear but compressed middle strokes do not select the abbreviation. Alternatives: quadr., qcq.. Confidence: medium.

**cancelled pantalon ending** — Reading: `\deleted{``\unsure{pantalon|pantalons}''}`. Strike covers the terminal ending; singular/plural is unresolved. Alternatives: pantalon, pantalons. Confidence: low.

**live triade ending** — Reading: `\unsure{triades|triade}`. Final low stroke may be s or a word exit before the point. Alternatives: triades, triade. Confidence: medium.

### Still unresolved

**third entry of opening triple** — Reading: `\deleted{\unsure{S}}`. Crossing strokes obscure the underform; two curves favor S without fully resolving it. Alternatives: S, unreadable canceled symbol. Confidence: low.

**last word of opening line** — Reading: `\unsure{où|ou}`. The word is legible as ou, but a detached grave accent is not securely distinguishable. Alternatives: où, ou. Confidence: medium.

**canceled adjective after X** — Reading: `\deleted{\unsure{close}}`. The tall middle stroke and low loops permit close; the hatching obscures individual letters. Alternatives: close, unreadable canceled word. Confidence: low.

**small trace before canceled S** — Reading: `\begin{tikzpicture}[x=.13cm,y=.13cm,baseline=-.1ex,line width=.5pt]
\draw (.08,.05) .. controls (-.03,.42) and (.24,.67) .. (.62,.89)
 .. controls (.97,.97) and (1.02,.7) .. (.72,.48)
 .. controls (.47,.28) and (.3,.08) .. (.08,.05);
\draw (.12,.15)--(.81,1.08);
\draw (.32,.04)--(.98,.78);
\draw (.11,.62)--(.83,.13);
\end{tikzpicture}`. The physically indistinct crossed trace cannot be assigned a letter. Its observed strokes are reproduced directly. Alternatives: . Confidence: low.

**large canceled S before finite R** — Reading: `\deleted{\unsure{S}}`. The large curved underform is too crossed for a secure S reading. Alternatives: S, unreadable canceled symbol. Confidence: low.

**canceled group after finite Sigma** — Reading: `\deleted{\unsure{\widetilde\Sigma}}`. The tentative tilde-Sigma underform remains heavily obscured by diagonal strokes. Alternatives: \widetilde\Sigma, unreadable canceled symbol. Confidence: low.

**M0686, left-margin connective** — Reading: `\unsure{mais}`. Compressed middle strokes do not permit every letter to be certified. Alternatives: mais, unreadable connective. Confidence: low.

**small canceled group after Variante** — Reading: `\unsure{de}`. The raised group has a loop and a short trailing stroke, but its cancellation obscures the reading. Alternatives: de, unreadable canceled word. Confidence: low.

**old S before R in vertex sentence** — Reading: `\deleted{\unsure{S}}`. The live R is clear; the adjacent canceled curves are only tentatively S. Alternatives: S, unreadable canceled symbol. Confidence: low.

**old vertex designation** — Reading: `\deleted{\unsure{pts de marquage}}`. The p descender and long marquage-like body survive, but some short internal letters are crossed. Alternatives: pts de marquage, partly unreadable canceled phrase. Confidence: low.

**canceled components word before trous** — Reading: `\unsure{composantes}`. The long connected crossed body permits the full word, but the overlapping crops do not certify all strokes of its raised ending. Alternatives: composantes, compos.. Confidence: low.

**M0687, first-row Sigma value** — Reading: `\unsure{\varnothing}`. A thick oval with crossing strokes favors an empty-set sign; retracing and cancellation cannot be fully separated. Alternatives: \varnothing, unresolved overtraced sign. Confidence: low.

**canceled first-row condition** — Reading: `\deleted{\unsure{R=\widetilde\Sigma=\varnothing}}`. Hatching obscures the middle expression and exact cancellation underform. Alternatives: R=\widetilde\Sigma=\varnothing, partly unreadable canceled condition. Confidence: low.

**older second-row canceled cluster** — Reading: `\deleted{\unsure{\partial X}}`. The dense earlier group permits partial X, but the underlying strokes are not fully separable. Alternatives: \partial X, unreadable canceled group. Confidence: low.

**second-row current condition and X status** — Reading: `\unsure{\partial X|\varnothing\deleted{X}}`. The oval with a tall stem is followed by a substantial X-like glyph. Current partial X is favored; an empty-set replacement with surviving canceled X remains possible because the cancellation boundary is uncertain. Alternatives: \partial X, \varnothing\deleted{X}. Confidence: low.

**old top subscript in third row** — Reading: `\deleted{\unsure{\mathrm{top}}}`. Small crossed t/o/p-like strokes lie beneath T and partly under D; not all letters are separable. Alternatives: \mathrm{top}, unreadable canceled subscript. Confidence: low.

**M0688, Sigma value in Cas comment** — Reading: `\unsure{\varnothing}`. The oval and intersecting strokes are thick and locally overtraced, as in the first-row value. Alternatives: \varnothing, unresolved overtraced sign. Confidence: low.

**surname in MD note** — Reading: `\unsure{Mumford}`. The sequence after f is compressed and does not securely separate o from r. The first reading is retained without normalization. Alternatives: Mumford, partly unreadable surname. Confidence: medium.

**initial canceled Dé** — Reading: `\deleted{\unsure{Dé}}`. The small initial D-like group and following accented stroke are crossed. Alternatives: Dé, unreadable canceled word opening. Confidence: low.

**canceled MD before bipondérés** — Reading: `\deleted{\unsure{MD}}`. The D bowl is visible; multiple cancellation strokes obscure the M. Alternatives: MD, unreadable canceled initials. Confidence: low.

### Still unresolved

**Header cancelled initials** — Reading: `\deleted{\unsure{\mathrm{MD}}}`. The left capital is crossed; D is clearer than M. Alternatives: MD, unrecoverable earlier left capital followed by D. Confidence: medium.

**First-map earlier label** — Reading: `\deleted{\unsure{g}}`. Hatching obscures the original loop/descender junction. Alternatives: g, another crossed looped label. Confidence: medium.

**Second-map cancelled domain** — Reading: `\deleted{\unsure{\vec A^\sigma|\vec A}}`. Vector arrow is certain, but a distinct raised sigma cannot be isolated from the cancellation. Alternatives: \vec A^\sigma, \vec A. Confidence: medium.

**Second-map crossed label and position of 2** — Reading: `r\,\deleted{\unsure{\rho}}\,2`. The crossed loop is rho-like; the surviving digit 2 is near r baseline, but its prior relation to the cancelled form cannot be fixed. Alternatives: r\,\deleted{\rho}\,2, r\,\deleted{\rho}_{2}. Confidence: medium.

**Caption short cancelled fragment** — Reading: `\deleted{\unsure{rigi}}`. Small dots and descender remnants support only a tentative short run. Alternatives: rigi, rig with further crossed remnant. Confidence: low.

**Caption longer cancelled word** — Reading: `\deleted{\unsure{rigidification}}`. Long horizontal cancellation erases much of the body; f/g-like descenders alone do not certify every letter. Alternatives: rigidification, partially unreadable longer word. Confidence: low.

**Crossed gloss** — Reading: `\deleted{``\unsure{arêtes libres}''}`. Several letter bodies lie under diagonal hatching. Alternatives: arêtes libres, partly unrecoverable gloss. Confidence: medium.

**Crossed lower relation** — Reading: `\deleted{\unsure{A_\ell}\overset{\mathrm{def}}{\subset}\vec A/\sigma}`. Left term remains uncertain under the strike. Def is drawn above the inclusion, between lines; exact attachment is a placement judgement. Alternatives: A_\ell\overset{\mathrm{def}}{\subset}\vec A/\sigma, def as a detached between-line annotation. Confidence: medium.

**Crossed quoted phrase** — Reading: `ens. des \unsure{sommets supplémentaires}`. Ens. is clear, but crossing strokes obscure parts of the following two words and their line break. Alternatives: sommets supplémentaires, partly unrecoverable quoted description. Confidence: medium.

**Slanted note capitalization** — Reading: `\unsure{La|la}`. The initial joining stroke and loop do not decisively distinguish a capital L from the small l form in this slant. Alternatives: La, la. Confidence: medium.

**Second marginal T** — Reading: `\omega(\unsure{T|T_a})\simeq\omega(a)`. No separately bounded a is secure in the dark lower-right stroke inside the first parentheses. Alternatives: T, T_a. Confidence: medium.

**Cancelled proposal interlinear word** — Reading: `\inserted{\unsure{apparents|marqués}}`. Two early downward strokes and the final rise fit apparents, while crossing strokes leave a marqués-like alternative viable. Alternatives: apparents, marqués. Confidence: low.

**Cancelled proposal local deletion** — Reading: `\deleted{\unsure{marqués}}`. The local horizontal strike runs across the word body. Alternatives: marqués, partly unreadable crossed word. Confidence: medium.

**Cancelled proposal libres** — Reading: ```\unsure{libres}''`. The beginning and ending are crossed by the large cancellation and boundary line. Alternatives: libres, partly unreadable quoted word. Confidence: medium.

**Cancelled proposal inserted element** — Reading: `\unsure{\alpha|a}\in A_\ell`. The small rounded element has a short right rising stroke; Latin a and alpha remain graphically compatible. Alternatives: \alpha, a. Confidence: medium.

**Marginal cancelled right term** — Reading: `\deleted{\unsure{S_\ell}}`. The upper body is densely retraced; ell survives separately, but the leading S cannot be certified. Alternatives: S_\ell, another crossed leading capital with ell. Confidence: low.

**Marginal crossed word** — Reading: `\deleted{\unsure{apparents}}`. Two early descenders and terminal t/s-like strokes survive, with middle letters hidden by the horizontal strike. Alternatives: apparents, partly unrecoverable crossed word. Confidence: medium.
